You can copy backups across accounts and Regions, share vaults for recovery using AWS Resource Access Manager (RAM), and protect vault access during account compromise with Multi-party approval. AWS announces Context Ontology Accelerator, an open source accelerator that helps organizations build an ontology of their business — a machine-readable model of their products, customers, policies, and the rules that define how they operate so AI agents make accurate, consistent, explainable, and auditable decisions. Context Ontology Accelerator connects to your structured and/or unstructured data sources, and uses AI to draft an ontology; your domain experts review, edit, and approve every element of it. The approved ontology is stored in a knowledge graph you own, expressed in open W3C standards so it works with any standards-based tooling, and any agent can consume it through the included Model Context Protocol (MCP) server.\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cbr\u003e\nOrganizations building AI agents need more trust in their agents\u0027 decisions, with outputs they can explain and audit. The context those agents require — entities, rules, policies, relationships — is scattered across dozens of systems with conflicting schemas and naming conventions. Teams spend months reconciling definitions and encoding them into prompts and point integrations, and because the results are not traceable, teams block agents from moving from proof-of-concept to production. Context Ontology Accelerator reduces what would take months of manual ontology authoring into days — from connecting data to serving governed answers to agents.\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cbr\u003e\nContext Ontology Accelerator is available today on GitHub under Apache 2.0. The initial release uses Amazon Neptune as the graph store, Amazon OpenSearch Serverless as the vector store, and foundation models hosted on Amazon Bedrock; deploy it yourself or engage AWS Professional Services. Context Ontology Accelerator’s managed, user-defined ontology capability will become a fully managed feature native to AWS Context. Customers who get started with Context Ontology Accelerator to create ontologies will be able to use and manage them with AWS Context. href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/neptune/\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eAmazon Neptune\u003c/a\u003e Database now supports tag-based access control (TBAC) for IAM, enabling customers to use AWS resource tags and IAM principal tags as conditions in IAM policies and Service Control Policies (SCPs) to control access to Neptune data-plane operations. Neptune already provides robust security through VPC isolation, TLS encryption, and IAM authentication, but customers managing multiple clusters at scale needed a dynamic, attribute-based mechanism to enforce organizational access boundaries. TBAC addresses this by allowing administrators to govern cluster access without enumerating specific cluster ARNs in every policy.\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cbr\u003e\nWith TBAC, IAM principals can only perform `neptune-db:*` actions against Neptune clusters whose tags match their own — for example, a principal tagged `Project\u003dFraudDetection` is automatically restricted to clusters sharing that same tag. This eliminates lateral access risk within shared VPC environments, enforces team and environment-level isolation across projects, and supports federated identity workflows using SAML or OIDC session tags from external identity providers. Granular permissions like neptune-db:QueryLanguage can be used alongside TBAC for more fine-grained access control.\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cbr\u003e\nThis feature is available in all AWS Regions where Amazon Neptune is available and requires Neptune engine version 1.2.0.0 or later with IAM authentication enabled.\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003cbr\u003e\nTo learn more about configuring tag-based access control for Amazon Neptune, including how to tag your DB clusters and IAM principals and deploy organization-wide guardrails using SCPs, visit the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/iam-data-tbac.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eAmazon Neptune documentation\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n","headlineUrl":"/about-aws/whats-new/2026/07/amazon-neptune-tbac/","postDateTime":"2026-07-27T22:00:00Z","headline":"Amazon Neptune now supports tag-based Spatial support provides 11 built-in spatial functions following the ISO 13249-3 standard, supporting GEOMETRY data types with seamless integration into existing GIS applications like \u003ca href\u003d\"https://www.esri.com/en-us/arcgis/geospatial-platform/overview\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eEsri ArcGIS\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eWith spatial support, customers no longer need to maintain separate spatial databases alongside their graph databases. Organizations can now perform proximity analysis, track asset locations and routes in network infrastructures, analyze geographic patterns in connected data, and build location-aware AI assistants that provide factual spatial answers without hallucinations. The feature particularly benefits mapping and navigation companies, ridesharing and transportation services, logistics providers, utilities, smart city planners, and emergency services organizations. Key capabilities include support for points, lines, and polygons, with functions like ST_DistanceSpheroid, ST_Within, ST_Intersects, and ST_Buffer, all compatible with openCypher query language.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eSpatial data support is available at no additional charge in all regions where Amazon Neptune Database is currently offered. With Neptune I/O-Optimized, there are zero charges for read and write I/O operations—you only pay for your database instances and storage usage, making it easy to predict your database spend up front. Neptune I/O-Optimized offers up to 40% cost savings for I/O-intensive applications where I/O charges exceed 25% of the total Neptune database spend.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eYou can now choose between two configurations for Amazon Neptune Database: Neptune Standard or Neptune I/O-Optimized. For applications with low-to-moderate I/Os, Neptune Standard is a cost-effective option. You can switch your cluster with a single click in the AWS Management Console or with a command through the AWS Command Line Interface. Neptune I/O-Optimized configuration is supported on from Neptune version 1.3.0.0 and provisioned instances. Neptune Serverless automatically determines and provisions the compute and memory resources to run the graph database, and scales capacity based on the workload’s changing requirements to maintain consistent performance. For example, Neptune Serverless automatically provisions and adjusts resources required to run a fraud graph as the query volumes processed vary. Neptune Serverless reduces costs by up to 90% compared with provisioning for peak capacity. With this launch, customers can effortlessly browse either labeled property graphs (LPG) or Resource Description Framework (RDF) data in a graph database and discover connections between data without having to write graph queries.\u0026nbsp;\u003cbr\u003e\r\n\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eThe graph-explorer open source tool provides a React-based web application that can be deployed as a Docker image to visualize graph data. You can connect to Amazon Neptune or another graph database that provides an Apache TinkerPop Gremlin or SPARQL 1.1 endpoint. You can search the data quickly using faceted search filters and interactively explore connections around nodes and edges. href\u003d\"https://www.w3.org/Submission/CBD/#:~:text\u003dGiven%20a%20particular%20node%20in,denoted%20by%20that%20particular%20node.\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eW3C RDF CBD (Concise Bounded Description)\u003c/a\u003e for the SPARQL query language. The Concise Bounded Description of an RDF resource (that is, a node in an RDF graph) is the smallest subgraph of leaf nodes that are not blank (anonymous resource, a node with no URI). A CBD query starts from a node, then recursively traverses the graph beyond blank nodes until it finds a node with an identifier. \u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eFor example, a CBD in a patient 360 healthcare application can be used to describe all the information related to a patient (a node). Here, the CBD query is a way to break the graph into small subgraphs that could be handled as \u0027objects\u0027, to facilitate the retrieval or to transactionally change its content.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eTo get started with CBD queries, visit the Amazon Neptune \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/sparql-query-hints-for-describe.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eSPARQL DESCRIBE documentation\u003c/a\u003e. with \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/neptune/\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eAmazon Neptune\u003c/a\u003e version 1.2.0.2, customers can now use real-time inductive learning (ML) predictions on nodes, edges and properties (entities) that were added to the graph after the ML model training process. With this launch, customers can make predictions on new data without requiring an update to their ML models. Amazon Neptune ML is powered by Amazon SageMaker, and uses Graph Neural Networks (GNNs), a machine learning technique purpose-built for graph that can improve the accuracy of most predictions for graphs by over 50% when compared to making predictions using non-graph methods based on published research from Stanford University.\u003cbr\u003e\r\n\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eCustomers often need real-time predictions for use cases like fraud detection, product recommendations, and identity resolution. With real time inductive inference for Neptune ML, customers can get near real-time predictions on new data by using existing Neptune ML models without retraining their ML models each time. Additionally, customers can now train and deploy Neptune ML models faster and save costs by training on a representative sample of their graph data and then deploying it to make predictions on any entity in the graph.\u003cbr /\u003e\r\n\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eTo learn more about real-time inductive inference, see the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/machine-learning-overview.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eNeptune ML documentation page\u003c/a\u003e. Neptune Serverless is a new deployment option that automatically scales capacity based on the needs of the application, making it easy and cost effective for developers to run graph databases without managing database capacity. Neptune is a fast, reliable, and fully managed graph database service for building and running applications with highly connected datasets, such as knowledge graphs, fraud graphs, identity graphs, and security graphs. With Neptune Serverless, you can run applications built using a graph database with a just few steps, and scale automatically to meet your application’s needs.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eIf you have unpredictable and variable workloads, Neptune Serverless automatically determines and provisions the compute and memory resources to run the graph database. In the unlikely event of a regional degradation or outage, one of the secondary regions can be promoted to full read/write capabilities.\u0026nbsp;You can have up-to five secondary regions with global database, and each secondary region can have up-to 16 replica instances.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eYou can create a Neptune Global Database with just a few clicks on the Amazon Neptune \u003ca href\u003d\"https://console.aws.amazon.com/neptune/\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eManagement Console\u003c/a\u003e or download the latest \u003ca href\u003d\"https://awscli.amazonaws.com/v2/documentation/api/latest/reference/neptune/index.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\" rel\u003d\"noopener\"\u003eAWS SDK or CLI.\u003c/a\u003e For Neptune Global Database, standard pricing will apply for the Neptune resources you use in all AWS Regions. Neptune Read Replicas share the same underlying volume as the primary instance and are well suited for read scaling. With Neptune Auto Scaling, you can specify a desired value for CloudWatch (CW) metrics for your Replicas such as average CPU utilization. Neptune Auto Scaling adjusts the number of Read Replicas to keep the CW metric closest to the value you specify.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eFor example, an increase in traffic could cause the average CPU utilization of your Replicas to go up and beyond your specified value. Customers can use the openCypher and Gremlin query languages together over the same property graph data. Support for openCypher is compatible with the Bolt protocol, enabling customers to continue to run applications that use the Bolt protocol to connect to Neptune. \u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eopenCypher is available in lab mode in this release and is enabled by default. The \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/ec2/instance-types/r5/\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eR5d instance type\u003c/a\u003e is based on the Amazon EC2 Nitro System and includes local NVMe-based SSD block level storage. Neptune R5d instances introduce a lookup cache that leverages the low-latency NVMe SSD storage to improve read query performance and reduce data retrievals from storage.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eFor use cases with frequent, repetitive lookups of property values or RDF literals, R5d instances use the lookup cache to temporarily store these values in the NVMe SSD volume for faster retrieval in the future. R5d instances can be deployed in the same Neptune cluster as R5 instances, enabling customers tailor the workload to the use case and customizing the compute instance needed for specific types of graph workloads. When the lookup cache is fully warmed with repeated access of property values or RDF literals, Neptune R5d instances can also observe a decrease in the amount of data read from storage and achieve better memory utilization.\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eR5d instances are supported with Amazon Neptune database running engine version 1.0.4.2 and higher. The lookup cache is only available on R5d instances, where it is automatically enabled by \u003cspan style\u003d\"\"\u003edefault.\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\r\n\u003cp\u003eTo learn more about the lookup cache and check if your use case could benefit, refer to the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/feature-overview-lookup-cache.html\" target\u003d\"_blank\"\u003eAmazon Neptune User Guide\u003c/a\u003e. Custom endpoints allow you to distribute your workload across a designated set of instances within a Neptune cluster.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eWith custom endpoints, you can specify which instances an endpoint refers to within a cluster and you choose what purpose the endpoint serves. For example, you can use a custom endpoint to route workloads that require higher processing power to a dedicated set of high-memory instances. This keeps other instances in the cluster isolated from these workloads.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e                   When you create a custom endpoint, you choose the instances to include in the endpoint. You can also configure whether newly created replicas in the cluster are added to your custom endpoint. You can add or delete instances from the custom endpoint at any time. You can continue to use the reader endpoint to distribute your read workload to all the replicas in the cluster, the cluster endpoint to connect to the writer instance, and instance endpoints to connect to a specific instance in your cluster. You can create up to five custom endpoints per Neptune cluster, and each endpoint must be associated with at least one database instance. \u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e                   Custom endpoints are available in \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/global-infrastructure/regional-product-services/\"\u003eregions\u003c/a\u003e where Neptune is available. AWS customers are interested in building applications that run in more than one public \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/global-infrastructure/\"\u003eAWS Region\u003c/a\u003e. Now customers can use cross-region snapshot copies with Neptune to simplify and streamline the data manipulation operations associated with building and running global graph applications using both Apache TinkerPop Gremlin and RDF/SPARQL.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e                   Neptune creates a storage volume snapshot of your cluster, backing up the entire DB cluster and not just individual databases. To copy the snapshot in a different region, select the snapshot, choose \u003cb\u003eCopy Snapshot\u003c/b\u003e from the \u003cb\u003eSnapshot \u0026gt; Actions\u003c/b\u003e menu, pick a destination region, and enter a name for the new snapshot. When you copy a snapshot to an AWS Region that is different from the source snapshot\u0027s AWS Region, the first copy is a full snapshot copy, even if you copy an incremental snapshot.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eYou can initiate the copy from the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://console.aws.amazon.com/neptune/home\"\u003eAWS Management Console\u003c/a\u003e, the \u003ca href\u003d\"http://aws.amazon.com/cli/\"\u003eAWS Command Line Interface (CLI)\u003c/a\u003e, or through the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/api-snapshots.html\"\u003eAmazon Neptune APIs\u003c/a\u003e. There is no charge for the copy operation itself; you pay only for the data transfer out of the source region and for the data storage in the destination region. You are not charged if the copy fails, but you are charged if you cancel a snapshot that is underway at the time. Refer our \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/backup-restore-copy-snapshot.html\"\u003edocumentation\u003c/a\u003e for more information.\u003c/p\u003e\n","modifiedDate":"2019-12-20T20:06:00Z","headlineUrl":"/about-aws/whats-new/2019/12/amazon-neptune-supports-cross-region-snapshot-copying/","postDateTime":"2019-12-19T12:19:03Z","contentType":"Launch Announcement","headline":"Amazon Neptune supports Cross-region Snapshot Copying","postSummary":"\u003cp\u003eAmazon Neptune now supports copying snapshots (created either automatically or manually) across regions. href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/neptune\"\u003eAmazon Neptune\u003c/a\u003e now supports \u003ca href\u003d\"https://www.w3.org/TR/sparql11-federated-query/\"\u003eSPARQL 1.1 Federated Query\u003c/a\u003e, a World Wide Web Consortium (W3C) Recommendation. Using Neptune customers can execute a portion of their query across different SPARQL endpoints within their Virtual Private Cloud (VPC), combine the result, and return to the user. Now customers can distribute their data across multiple Neptune clusters and using a single query to access that data across those clusters. Customers can also use federation to combine data in Neptune with data from external SPARQL endpoints.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp class\u003d\"MsoNormal\"\u003eSPARQL 1.1 Federated Query uses the SPARQL \u003cspan style\u003d\"font-family: Consolas; mso-bidi-font-family: \u0026quot;Times New Roman\u0026quot;; mso-bidi-theme-font: minor-bidi;\"\u003eservice \u003c/span\u003ekeyword. The keyword included in the query identifies the location of the SPARQL endpoint to retrieve the data. Neptune SPARQL federation can also be enabled to use federate against another Neptune cluster that is secured by the AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) service. Customers need to ensure the federated SPARQL endpoint adheres to the data model and security requirements.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThere are no additional charges to use federated query as long as the data transfer is within the VPC. Customers can try out federated query today. Please refer to the \u003ca href\u003d\"https://docs.aws.amazon.com/neptune/latest/userguide/sparql-service.html\"\u003eAmazon Neptune User Guide\u003c/a\u003e for more details on using SPARQL Service.\u003c/p\u003e\n","modifiedDate":"2019-10-16T21:45:21Z","headlineUrl":"/about-aws/whats-new/2019/10/amazon-neptune-now-supports-SPARQL-11-federated-query/","postDateTime":"2019-10-16T13:57:00Z","contentType":"Launch Announcement","headline":"Amazon Neptune now supports SPARQL 1.1 federated query","postSummary":"\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/neptune\"\u003eAmazon Neptune\u003c/a\u003e now supports \u003ca href\u003d\"https://www.w3.org/TR/sparql11-federated-query/\"\u003eSPARQL 1.1 Federated Query\u003c/a\u003e, a World Wide Web Consortium (W3C) Recommendation. Using Neptune customers can execute a portion of their query across different SPARQL endpoints within their Virtual Private Cloud (VPC), combine the result, and return to the user. Now customers can distribute their data across multiple Neptune clusters and using a single query to access that data across those clusters. Customers can also use federation to combine data in Neptune with data from external SPARQL endpoints.\u003c/p\u003e\n"}},"tags":[{"id":"GLOBAL#content-type#launch-announcement","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"GLOBAL#content-type","name":"Launch Announcement","description":"Launch Announcement","dateCreated":"2020-04-29T05:19:27+0000","dateUpdated":"2022-02-03T03:31:08+0000"},{"id":"whats-new-v2#year#2019","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#year","name":"2019","description":"year#2019","dateCreated":"2023-11-06T18:17:49+0000","dateUpdated":"2023-11-06T18:17:49+0000"},{"id":"whats-new-v2#general-products#aws-govcloud-us","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#general-products","name":"aws-govcloud-us","description":"general:products/aws-govcloud-us","dateCreated":"2023-11-07T18:41:18+0000","dateUpdated":"2023-11-07T18:41:18+0000"},{"id":"whats-new-v2#general-products#amazon-neptune","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#general-products","name":"amazon-neptune","description":"general:products/amazon-neptune","dateCreated":"2023-11-09T13:51:51+0000","dateUpdated":"2023-11-09T13:51:51+0000"},{"id":"whats-new-v2#marketing-marchitecture#databases","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#marketing-marchitecture","name":"databases","description":"marketing:marchitecture/databases","dateCreated":"2023-11-07T18:00:12+0000","dateUpdated":"2023-11-07T18:00:12+0000"}]},{"item":{"id":"whats-new-v2#about-aws-whats-new-2019-10-amazon-neptune-now-supports-streams-to-capture-graph-data-changes","locale":"en_US","directoryId":"whats-new-v2","name":"/about-aws/whats-new/2019/10/amazon-neptune-now-supports-streams-to-capture-graph-data-changes/","dateCreated":"2023-11-09T14:19:45+0000","dateUpdated":"2024-07-25T13:16:41+0000","additionalFields":{"postBody":"\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/neptune\"\u003eAmazon Neptune\u003c/a\u003e now supports Streams, an easy way to capture changes in your graph. When enabled, Neptune Streams logs changes to your graph (change-log data) as they happen. Neptune Streams are useful when you want to notify processes (e.g. trigger a lambda) as changes occur in your graph. Streams can also be useful to maintain a current version of your graph in a different region or service such as the Amazon Elasticsearch Service, Amazon ElastiCache, or Amazon Simple Storage Service (S3).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eNeptune Streams is available in lab mode in this release. Lab mode allows you to test a feature before they are available in production. Neptune Streams can be enabled or disabled in lab mode using the database cluster parameter neptune_lab_mode. Once enabled, you can access Neptune Streams using the HTTP GET requests to REST APIs /sparql/streams or /gremlin/streams. The response will be a JSON feed of the operations and the changes to the graph.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e                   When Neptune Streams are enabled, you incur I/O and storage charges associated with the change-log data. Change records are purged automatically a week after they are created. You can try out Neptune Streams today.                   You can now use \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/neptune/\"\u003eAmazon Neptune\u003c/a\u003e in applications that are subject to Service Organization Control (SOC)                 You can now use \u003ca href\u003d\"https://aws.amazon.com/neptune/\"\u003eAmazon Neptune\u003c/a\u003e in applications that are subject to PCI compliance or require ISO certification. The core of Amazon Neptune is a purpose-built, high-performance graph database engine optimized for storing billions of relationships and querying the graph with milliseconds latency. Amazon Neptune supports popular graph models Apache TinkerPop and W3C\u0027s RDF, and their associated query languages TinkerPop Gremlin and RDF SPARQL, allowing you to easily build queries that efficiently navigate highly connected datasets. Neptune powers graph use cases such as recommendation engines, fraud detection, knowledge graphs, drug discovery, and network security.\u003cbr\u003e\n\u003c/p\u003e\n"}},"tags":[{"id":"whats-new-v2#year#2017","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#year","name":"2017","description":"year#2017","dateCreated":"2023-11-09T14:16:58+0000","dateUpdated":"2023-11-09T14:16:58+0000"},{"id":"GLOBAL#content-type#launch-announcement","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"GLOBAL#content-type","name":"Launch Announcement","description":"Launch Announcement","dateCreated":"2020-04-29T05:19:27+0000","dateUpdated":"2022-02-03T03:31:08+0000"},{"id":"whats-new-v2#general-products#amazon-neptune","locale":"en_US","tagNamespaceId":"whats-new-v2#general-products","name":"amazon-neptune","description":"general:products/amazon-neptune","dateCreated":"2023-11-09T13:51:51+0000","dateUpdated":"2023-11-09T13:51:51+0000"}]}],"metadata":{"count":136,"totalHits":136},"fieldTypes":{"regionalAvailability":"LongText","nofollow":"Boolean","date":"Date","language":"Text","title":"Text","postDateTime":"DateTime","body":"Text","postSummary":"LongText","ctaLabel":"Text","mediaThumbnail":"Text","nosnippet":"Boolean","price":"Text","ctaLink":"URL","headline":"Text","contentType":"Text","bodyBack":"LongText","secondaryCTALabel":"Text","primaryCTALink":"URL","noindex":"Boolean","level":"Text","heading":"Text","mediaType":"Text","mediaAlt":"Text","headlineUrl":"Text","mediaSrc":"URL","durationMinutes":"Number","badge":"JSON","videoImageUrl":"URL","postBody":"LongText","secondaryCTALink":"URL","Video":"URL","modifiedDate":"DateTime","namePerson":"Text","location":"Text","time":"Text","publishedDate":"DateTime","durationReadtime":"Number","primaryCTALabel":"Text"}}
